PD2 Gas Lift Design: TEACHING DIGEST

THE ONLY NUMBERS A LESSON MAY QUOTE. Every line names its source: a PUBLISHED
golden case out of packages/engines/test-data/production/goldens/gaslift_cases.json,
a DERIVED sweep run on published inputs, or one of the two TEACHING constructs
this wave designed for itself. NOTHING in this file comes from the graded
capstone well. The capstone conditions and its eighteen graded answers live in
a separate file that the generator of this digest never opens.

A leak guard, pd2_leakcheck.mjs, reads the capstone graded field list and every
number in every line of this file, and rejects the digest if any number lands
within TEN TIMES a graded field tolerance of that field value, in three unit
shiftings (as printed, times 1000, times 0.001). academy_submit_capstone grades
with abs(got - expected) <= tol, so tol is ABSOLUTE in the field own units and
is not a fraction of anything: ten times it is under a hundredth of a psi on a
pressure and under a twentieth of a foot on a depth. This file passed that
guard, and the closest approach anywhere in it is over two hundred tolerances
away, on a PUBLISHED golden valve setting that this digest is obliged to carry.

Generator: /root/pd-wip-gaslift/pd2_dump.mjs
Engine:    packages/engines/engines/production/gasLiftDesign.js
           packages/engines/engines/production/gasLiftValves.js
           packages/engines/engines/production/gasProperties.js
Goldens:   packages/engines/test-data/production/goldens/gaslift_cases.json
Golden oracle: tools/validation/production/oracle_gaslift.py
Units: psia, Mscf/d, ft TVD, degF, psi/ft, in, in2, lbm/ft3. Never psig, never SI.

WHAT PROVENANCE LABEL MEANS WHAT
  golden ...    a value committed in gaslift_cases.json, cut by the independent
                stdlib oracle (RK4 column, bisection roots) named above.
  engine ...    the shipped engine re-run on published golden inputs. Where a
                golden carries the same quantity, the two are reported together
                so a lesson can see the agreement or the divergence.
  derived ...   a sweep or refinement run on PUBLISHED inputs by this generator.
                A sweep point is not a published case. Say so if you print one.
  teaching ...  a construct this wave invented: the teaching well AKASO-3 and the
                teaching traverse on it. Not a published case, not a real well,
                and never to be presented as either.

THE FOUR PUBLISHED DESIGN CASES AT A GLANCE
  westTexasOil
      8000 ft oil well, 1000 psig kickoff, 1.5 in valves, 25 psi per valve.
      valves = 8, stop reason = targetDepth, multipointing stages = 2, 3, 4
  deepHighPressure
      11000 ft, 1400 psig kickoff, 1 in valves, 40 psi per valve, heavier kill fluid.
      valves = 7, stop reason = minSpacing, multipointing stages = none
  constantPressurePPO
      Constant surface pressure design with production-operated valves.
      valves = 6, stop reason = targetDepth, multipointing stages = none
  midDecrementKnifeEdge
      9000 ft, 1150 psig kickoff, 26.75 psi per valve: a decrement in the middle of the usual 20-50 psi band, where the unloading verdict is neither all-open nor all-shut. Valves 1 to 3 stay open one stage each and the string is clean from stage 6 down, and the stage-5 verdict on valve 4 hangs on a fraction of a psi of closing margin. It is here because the two extremes (a decrement far below every spread, and one far above) only test the direction of the rule, not the rule.
      valves = 7, stop reason = targetDepth, multipointing stages = 2, 3, 4, 5

THE ONE TEACHING WELL
  AKASO-3   a 7200 ft IPO string this wave designed so the same well can be run
            three ways, on the surface-close convention, on the constant-pressure
            convention, and as a PPO string, and so a curved flowing traverse
            exists whose exact crossing is known. It is a TEACHING WELL. It is
            not in the goldens and no oracle has ever checked it.

# SECTION 2: PUBLISHED GOLDEN GAS COLUMNS, DOWN AND BACK UP
# Three columns marched from surface to depth, and the same three inverted back
# to the surface pressure that produced them. Associate m02 l04 and l05 own the
# march and the inverse. The round trip closes to under a millionth of a psi,
# which is what a lesson should say about the inverse: it is a secant on the
# forward march, so it is exactly as good as the march it inverts and no better.

# SECTION 3: THE REAL GAS COLUMN AGAINST THE 0.02 PSI/FT RULE OF THUMB
# DERIVED from the published column cases. Associate m02 l03 and m05 l03 turn on
# this comparison, so print it rather than assert it. The engine header names the
# 0.02 psi/ft rule of thumb explicitly as the thing it does not do.
# READ THE SIGN CAREFULLY, because it is the whole lesson. The real gas gradient
# is rho / 144 and rho is proportional to p / (z T), so the gradient tracks the
# PRESSURE LEVEL of the column and not the depth. On the 1414.7 psia column it is
# close to twice the rule of thumb, on the 1014.7 psia column about a quarter
# above it, and on the 614.7 psia column about a third BELOW it. A flat rule has
# no pressure in it at all, so it is wrong in one direction on a high pressure
# system and wrong in the other direction on a low pressure one. A lesson that
# says only that the rule of thumb reads low has learned half of it.
# AND A SECOND THING, which contradicts the easy story. Down a real well the
# gradient DOES NOT simply grow with depth. Compression pushes it up and the
# rising geotherm pushes it down, and on all three published columns, which carry
# ordinary linear geotherms, the temperature wins: the local gradient FALLS a few
# percent from surface to packer. Hold the temperature constant and the same
# column shows the gradient rising, which is the isothermal block printed with
# each column below. The gradient grows with PRESSURE. Whether it grows with
# DEPTH is a race between two effects and it has to be computed, not assumed.
derived rule of thumb, column 1 (surface 1014.7 psia, sg 0.65) at 0.0 ft: engine column = 1014.700000 psia, flat 0.02 psi/ft rule = 1014.700000 psia, rule error = 0.000000 psi, engine local gradient = 0.025405143 psi/ft, local gradient over 0.02 = 1.270257 ratio, z = 0.869373540, temperature = 100.0000 degF

# SECTION 4: THE COLUMN CONVERGES. THE STEP REFINEMENT STUDY
# DERIVED from the published column cases and the published design packer depths.
# This is the honest negative result the module is built on, and Associate m05
# owns it. The march is a predictor with a trapezoidal corrector on the gradient,
# so it is second order: every doubling of the step count cuts the remaining error
# by about four. Print the SEQUENCE, not a summary of it, because the skill being
# taught is reading a convergence sequence and not trusting a claim about one.
# The engine calls this march internally at steps = 20 for spacing and valve
# settings, and at steps = 40 for the plotted injection curve, so the last two
# rows of each block are the number a design actually carries.

derived step refinement, THE LESSON: this column has no truncation problem. The
derived step refinement, error at the step count the engine actually uses is
derived step refinement, thousandths of a psi on systems of over a thousand psia,
derived step refinement, and the error ratio near 4.0 in every block says the
derived step refinement, method is behaving exactly as its order predicts. A
derived step refinement, learner who leaves believing every numerical method is
derived step refinement, suspect has learned the wrong lesson. The skill is
derived step refinement, telling the two cases apart, and the way you tell them
derived step refinement, apart is by refining and watching.

# SECTION 5: THE INJECTION PRESSURE CURVE AND ITS CHORD BIAS
# DERIVED from the published column cases. injectionPressureCurve tabulates the
# march at `steps` samples and then reads any depth by STRAIGHT LINE between two
# samples. That is a second source of error, separate from the march itself, and
# it is the one the deepest injection point later trips over. Everything here is
# measured against a converged 20000 step march of the same column.
# THREE NUMBERS PER ROW, and they mean different things.
#   deviation AT A SAMPLE is pure march truncation: at a tabulated node there is
#     no chord, so this is the error of the march at that step count.
#   deviation BETWEEN SAMPLES is truncation plus chord, which is what a reader of
#     the plotted curve actually suffers.
#   CHORD COMPONENT is the second minus the average of the deviations at the two
#     nodes that bracket it, so it isolates the straight line from the march.
# The chord component is NEGATIVE on these columns because their gradient falls
# slightly with depth, which makes the pressure curve concave, and a chord under a
# concave curve reads low. Associate m05 and Expert m04 both need this block.
derived chord bias, column 1 (surface 1014.7 psia, 8000 ft) at    4 samples: sample spacing = 2000.0000 ft, worst deviation anywhere = -3.6727e-2 psi at 960.0 ft, deviation AT the sample at 4000.0 ft = 7.1851e-3 psi, deviation BETWEEN samples at 5000.0 ft = -2.0920e-2 psi, CHORD COMPONENT = -2.9517e-2 psi
derived chord bias, column 1 (surface 1014.7 psia, 8000 ft) at    8 samples: sample spacing = 1000.0000 ft, worst deviation anywhere = -9.7606e-3 psi at 480.0 ft, deviation AT the sample at 4000.0 ft = 1.7990e-3 psi, deviation BETWEEN samples at 4500.0 ft = -5.6267e-3 psi, CHORD COMPONENT = -7.6086e-3 psi

derived chord bias, column 1, THE HEADLINE: the chord component falls by a factor near four for every doubling of the sample count, exactly like the march itself, and at the 64 sample resolution it is thousandths of a psi or smaller. It is NEGATIVE throughout, because these columns have a slightly falling gradient and a chord under a concave curve reads low.

# SECTION 6: THE ISOTHERMAL CLOSED FORM, THE MARCH SELF CHECK
# DERIVED. With z pinned at 1 and the temperature held constant the march has a
# closed form to reproduce, and reproducing it is the self asserting gate on the
# routine: a numerical method that cannot reproduce the one case it HAS a closed
# form for is not to be trusted on the cases it has none for.
#
# TWO CLOSED FORMS ARE PRINTED HERE AND THE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN THEM IS THE POINT.
# The textbook form carries the ROUNDED coefficient 0.01875. The engine carries
# its own constants, AIR_MW 28.9625 lbm/lbmol and R 10.7316 psia ft3 per lbmol
# degR, whose coefficient is AIR_MW / (144 R) = 0.0187417041, lower by 4.43e-4
# relative. So the march CANNOT converge onto the textbook form, and against it
# the error parks at a floor that refinement never removes.
#
# The first draft of this block printed only the textbook form and called the
# result convergence. It was not: the gate could not fail, because it was
# measuring a fixed constant mismatch. TWO lesson writers independently refined
# the march, watched the residual park, and reported it rather than writing
# around it. Against the ENGINE-CONSTANT form the march converges properly, to
# about 1e-7 psi at 2000 steps, and that number does keep shrinking.
#
# Both forms stay in the file. The parked residual is teaching material in its
# own right, and it is the cleanest demonstration in the wave of the difference
# between a truncation, which refinement removes, and a difference between two
# FORMULATIONS, which it never touches. Associate m02 l04 and m05 l04 use it.
derived closed form, engine molar mass of air AIR_MW = 28.9625 lbm per lbmol
derived closed form, engine gas constant R_UNIVERSAL = 10.7316 psia ft3 per lbmol degR
derived closed form, textbook coefficient = 0.0187500000
derived closed form, engine coefficient, AIR_MW over 144 R = 0.0187417041
derived closed form, the two coefficients differ by = 4.4264e-4 relative
derived closed form, surface 1014.7 psia, 8000 ft, sg 0.65, isothermal at 140.0 degF, z pinned at 1.0:
derived closed form, exact p(D) = pSurf * exp(0.01875 * sg * D / T) = 1193.848862916 psia
derived closed form, ENGINE-CONSTANT p(D) = pSurf * exp(AIR_MW / (144 R) * sg * D / T) = 1193.762984148 psia
derived closed form, the two closed forms differ by = 8.5879e-2 psi
derived closed form, march at    2 steps = 1193.869339003 psia, error against the closed form = 2.0476e-2 psi
derived closed form, march at    2 steps, error against the ENGINE-CONSTANT form = 1.0635e-1 psi
derived closed form, march at   10 steps = 1193.767253577 psia, error against the closed form = -8.1609e-2 psi
derived closed form, march at   10 steps, error against the ENGINE-CONSTANT form = 4.2694e-3 psi

# SECTION 7: PUBLISHED GOLDEN NITROGEN DOME CHARGES, AND A DOME AS A THERMOMETER
# The dome charge is set on a 60 degF test rack and then works at valve
# temperature. The correction is a fixed volume real gas ratio P/(z T) = constant
# on nitrogen z, and Ct = Pd60 / PdT. Professional m02 l03 owns this. The linear
# 1 + 0.00215 (T - 60) rule of thumb printed in older manuals is shown beside it,
# because the engine header names that rule of thumb as the thing it refuses.
golden nitrogen, 60 degF charge 600.0 psia at valve temperature 120.0 degF, dome at temperature = 675.573876944 psia
golden nitrogen, 60 degF charge 600.0 psia at valve temperature 120.0 degF, Ct = Pd60 / PdT = 0.888133808 dimensionless
golden nitrogen, 60 degF charge 600.0 psia at valve temperature 120.0 degF, z at 60 degF = 0.979414628, z at valve temperature = 0.988632778
engine nitrogen, 60 degF charge 600.0 psia at 120.0 degF, dome at temperature = 675.573876944 psia, difference from golden = -1.728e-11 psi
engine nitrogen, inverse from 675.573877 psia at 120.0 degF back to 60 degF = 600.000000000 psia, difference from the golden charge = 1.467e-11 psi
engine nitrogen, temperatureCorrectionFactor at 120.0 degF = 0.888133808, difference from golden Ct = 1.380e-13
derived nitrogen, the linear rule of thumb 1 / (1 + 0.00215 (T - 60)) at 120.0 degF = 0.885739593, real gas Ct = 0.888133808, rule of thumb error = -0.002394216 which is -0.2696 percent
derived nitrogen, the dome pressure the linear rule of thumb would predict at 120.0 degF = 677.400000 psia against the real gas 675.573877 psia, a miss of 1.826123 psi

derived nitrogen, a dome charge is therefore a THERMOMETER as much as a spring:
derived nitrogen, at a fixed 60 degF charge the pressure the valve actually feels
derived nitrogen, rises with valve depth because the valve gets hotter, and the
derived nitrogen, rule of thumb drifts further the hotter and the deeper it gets.

# westTexasOil unloading, stage by stage. The golden closingMargins come from the
# oracle, which evaluates the published closing rule (Takacs ch. 3, Brown vol. 2a,
# API Book 6) AT VALVE DEPTH off a forward RK4 column. The engine evaluates the
# same rule AT SURFACE by inverting a coarser column, so the two are different
# roads to the same verdict and a lesson may say so.

# deepHighPressure unloading, stage by stage. The golden closingMargins come from the
# oracle, which evaluates the published closing rule (Takacs ch. 3, Brown vol. 2a,
# API Book 6) AT VALVE DEPTH off a forward RK4 column. The engine evaluates the
# same rule AT SURFACE by inverting a coarser column, so the two are different
# roads to the same verdict and a lesson may say so.

# constantPressurePPO unloading, stage by stage. The golden closingMargins come from the
# oracle, which evaluates the published closing rule (Takacs ch. 3, Brown vol. 2a,
# API Book 6) AT VALVE DEPTH off a forward RK4 column. The engine evaluates the
# same rule AT SURFACE by inverting a coarser column, so the two are different
# roads to the same verdict and a lesson may say so.

# SECTION 12: PUBLISHED DESIGN CASE midDecrementKnifeEdge
# 9000 ft, 1150 psig kickoff, 26.75 psi per valve: a decrement in the middle of the usual 20-50 psi band, where the unloading verdict is neither all-open nor all-shut. Valves 1 to 3 stay open one stage each and the string is clean from stage 6 down, and the stage-5 verdict on valve 4 hangs on a fraction of a psi of closing margin. It is here because the two extremes (a decrement far below every spread, and one far above) only test the direction of the rule, not the rule.
golden design midDecrementKnifeEdge, input pKickoffPsia = 1164.7 psia
golden design midDecrementKnifeEdge, input pOperatingPsia = 1064.7 psia
golden design midDecrementKnifeEdge, input pWhUnloadPsia = 154.7 psia
golden design midDecrementKnifeEdge, input method = surfaceClose
golden design midDecrementKnifeEdge, input dpPerValvePsi = 26.75 psi per valve
golden design midDecrementKnifeEdge, input dpTransferPsi = 60.0 psi
golden design midDecrementKnifeEdge, input killGradPsiPerFt = 0.46 psi/ft
golden design midDecrementKnifeEdge, input unloadGradPsiPerFt = 0.09 psi/ft
golden design midDecrementKnifeEdge, input gasSg = 0.68
golden design midDecrementKnifeEdge, input maxDepthFt = 9000.0 ft, minSpacingFt = 275.0 ft, maxValves = 12
golden design midDecrementKnifeEdge, input wellhead temperature = 105.0 degF, bottom temperature = 215.0 degF at reference depth 9600.0 ft
golden design midDecrementKnifeEdge, input valveType = IPO, bellowsAreaIn2 = 0.77 in2, bottomOrifice = true, orificeIdIn = 0.3125
golden design midDecrementKnifeEdge, input port catalog = 0.25, 0.3125, 0.375, 0.4375, 0.5, 0.625, 0.75 in
golden design midDecrementKnifeEdge, input qgiTargetMscfd = 600.0 Mscf/d
golden design midDecrementKnifeEdge, stop reason = targetDepth
golden design midDecrementKnifeEdge, valve count = 7
golden design midDecrementKnifeEdge, valve 1 depth = 2354.019705701 ft, surface injection pressure at its stage = 1164.7000 psia

# midDecrementKnifeEdge unloading, stage by stage. The golden closingMargins come from the
# oracle, which evaluates the published closing rule (Takacs ch. 3, Brown vol. 2a,
# API Book 6) AT VALVE DEPTH off a forward RK4 column. The engine evaluates the
# same rule AT SURFACE by inverting a coarser column, so the two are different
# roads to the same verdict and a lesson may say so.

# SECTION 13: THE PPO DIVERGENCE, BOTH SIDES OF IT
# A PINNED KNOWN DIVERGENCE, not a bug to be worked around in a lesson and not a
# worked example. Professional m03 states it as a limit. Expert may cite it as the
# clearest case in the course of a verdict that is confident and wrong.
# The engine computes closingSurfacePressurePsia by taking the dome charge, which
# for a PPO valve balances against the TUBING, and inverting it up a CASING gas
# column. unloadingSequence then compares that number with the casing pressure. So
# a production operated string is closed on the wrong fluid. Not fixed, because the
# engine is consumed by a live Suite app.
engine PPO divergence, valve 1 at 2410.595757 ft: the engine casing side test compares the constant surface pressure 1114.7 psia with a closing surface pressure of 389.713022538 psia, so it clears the open threshold by 724.986977462 psi and the engine calls the valve OPEN
engine PPO divergence, valve 2 at 4236.835774 ft: the engine casing side test compares the constant surface pressure 1114.7 psia with a closing surface pressure of 503.252041656 psia, so it clears the open threshold by 611.447958344 psi and the engine calls the valve OPEN

# SECTION 14: THE TOP VALVE, AND THE FIXED POINT THAT FINDS IT
# DERIVED on published inputs. Valve 1 sits where the injection line first
# overcomes a full column of kill fluid above the unloading wellhead pressure.
# The depth depends on the injection pressure which depends on the depth, so the
# engine iterates. The first iterate is the answer you would get if gas were
# weightless, and the sequence shows exactly what the weight of the gas buys you.
# Associate m04 owns all of this.

# SECTION 15: SPACING IS A RECURSION, MADE VISIBLE
#
# PROVENANCE, AND IT MATTERS BECAUSE TWO SETS OF NUMBERS LIVE IN THIS FILE.
# The depths and increments below come from a STANDALONE RE-RUN of the spacing
# recursion, iterate by iterate, so a lesson can show it converging. Section 9
# prints the depths and increments the DESIGN ITSELF returned. The two agree to
# about seven significant figures and disagree after that: valve 1 to valve 2 is
# 1563.466592902 ft here and 1563.466503048 ft there, and the pattern repeats
# down the string. Neither is wrong. They are two roads to one quantity, and the
# fixed point is stopped at a tolerance rather than solved exactly.
#
# So do not pair a head from this section with an increment from section 9. A
# lesson writer reported the trap after keeping each of their lessons internally
# consistent on one road or the other, which is the right way to handle it.
#
# DERIVED on published westTexasOil inputs. Professional m01 owns this. Valve n
# sits where the injection line at the decremented surface pressure, less the
# transfer differential, still beats the transfer pressure at valve n-1, with kill
# fluid in between. Written out, valve n is the fixed point of
#   d = d(n-1) + ( pInj(pSurf(n), d) - dpTransfer - pProd(n-1) ) / killGrad
# and the engine iterates it to 0.01 ft. Here is that iteration, valve by valve.
derived spacing recursion, westTexasOil valve 2: surface pressure at this stage = 1014.7 - 1 x 25.00 = 989.7000 psia
derived spacing recursion, westTexasOil valve 2: valve 1 sits at 2119.249994721 ft and its transfer production pressure is 114.7 + 0.1 x 2119.249995 = 326.624999472 psia
derived spacing recursion, westTexasOil valve 2: iterate 0 (the minimum spacing seed) = 2369.249994721 ft

derived spacing recursion, THE SHAPE OF IT: the increments SHRINK on the way down,
derived spacing recursion, because the surface pressure is falling by a fixed
derived spacing recursion, amount per valve while the transfer pressure it has to
derived spacing recursion, beat is rising with depth. That is why a string runs out
derived spacing recursion, of room rather than running out of valves.

# SECTION 17: TWO CONVENTIONS, ONE RECURSION
# DERIVED. surfaceClose drops the surface injection pressure a fixed amount per
# valve, which is what makes the upper valves close as the point of injection moves
# down. constantPressure holds the surface pressure and leans on the transfer
# differential alone: the engine sets the decrement to zero and runs the SAME
# recursion. Professional m01 l04 owns this. The published constantPressurePPO case
# is the convention seen on its own; here it is seen as a difference, by running
# two published designs both ways.

# SECTION 18: WHERE SPACING STOPS, AND THE MINIMUM SPACING EXEMPTION
# Four stop reasons exist and three of them raise a warning. Professional m01 l05
# owns this. The exemption is a real engine behaviour and a lesson must state it.
# DEFECT (d), stated as a limit: when the recursion lands at or past the floor the
# engine pushes that mandrel to the floor and stops WITHOUT testing minSpacingFt,
# so the target-depth mandrel is exempt from the minimum the design declared.
golden stop reason, westTexasOil = targetDepth, last increment = 131.375432376 ft against a stated minSpacingFt of 250.0 ft, exempt = true
golden stop reason, deepHighPressure = minSpacing, last increment = 319.359713665 ft against a stated minSpacingFt of 300.0 ft, exempt = false
golden stop reason, constantPressurePPO = targetDepth, last increment = 152.956208919 ft against a stated minSpacingFt of 200.0 ft, exempt = true
golden stop reason, midDecrementKnifeEdge = targetDepth, last increment = 430.416867128 ft against a stated minSpacingFt of 275.0 ft, exempt = false
derived minSpacing exemption, the published westTexasOil case lands its last mandrel 131.375432376 ft from its neighbour against a stated 250 ft minimum, and no warning is raised, because the stop reason is targetDepth and that branch returns before the minSpacing test
derived minSpacing exemption, the published deepHighPressure case DOES stop on minSpacing, at 7 valves, its deepest mandrel at 8644.662255376 ft and so still 1855.337744624 ft short of its floor of 10500.0 ft. The next valve the recursion wanted would have sat inside the 300 ft minimum, so the string stopped and the minSpacing warning WAS raised. # SECTION 19: WHAT FLIPS THE MULTIPOINTING VERDICT
# DERIVED sweeps on the PUBLISHED midDecrementKnifeEdge case. Expert m02 and m05
# own this. That case is spaced on 26.75 psi per valve, a decrement in the middle of
# the usual 20 to 50 psi band, and its stage 5 verdict on valve 4 hangs on a fraction
# of a psi. The engine surface margin and the oracle valve-depth margin are two
# different numbers for the same knife edge, and BOTH are small, which is the point.
#
# THE MECHANISM, STATED OUTRIGHT, because it is more teachable than "hangs on a
# fraction of a psi" and the file only ever implied it. Across all six charged
# valves of this case the open flag IS the test of the casing DROP since a valve
# own opening stage against that valve SPREAD. Valve 4 opens with a spread of
# 32.272254090 psi against a drop of 32.122462454 psi, and the difference between
# those two numbers, 0.149791635 psi, is the whole knife edge. Section 12 carries
# the drop beside the spread on every valve, so the rule can be checked rather
# than believed. A lesson writer worked it out from those rows and said so.
#
# AND A TRAP THAT FOLLOWS FROM IT. The SURFACE decrement is 26.75 psi per valve,
# but the drop a valve actually feels AT DEPTH runs from 28.652797457 psi at valve
# 1 to 33.355029522 psi at valve 6. Compare a spread against the decrement rather
# than against the drop and you will pick the wrong valves.
golden knife edge, published stage 5 closing margin on valve 4 (oracle, at valve depth) = 0.149791635 psi
engine knife edge, published stage 5 surface margin on valve 4 (engine, at surface) = 0.124769727 psi
derived knife edge, the whole system runs at 1164.7 psia at surface and 1279.335785 psia at valve 4, so that verdict is a fraction of a psi on a system of over a thousand psia

# SECTION 20: THE DEEPEST INJECTION POINT, AND THE CHORD THAT CANNOT SEE ITSELF
# DEFECT (b). Expert m03 and m04 own this. deepestInjectionPoint locates the
# crossing between two tabulated traverse points by straight line on the margin,
# and it reads the injection pressure by straight line between two tabulated column
# samples. BOTH SIDES of the residual therefore come off the same pair of chords,
# so the residual it reports is a statement that the two chords agree with each
# other and nothing more. Refine the tabulation and the answer moves while the
# reported residual stays small.
#
# TWO RUNS LIVE IN THIS SECTION AND THEY ARE NOT THE SAME RUN. Read the labels.
#
#   THE ENGINE LINE, below, is the shipped answer as the shipped engine produces
#   it: the published 1000 ft tabulation AND the injection curve at the sample
#   count the engine defaults to. It reports -1.317711139 ft of depth error, a
#   residual of 4.6770e-3 psi, a true residual of 1.58211e-1 psi, and a ratio of
#   33.83. That is the number a user of the studio is actually handed.
#
#   THE REFINEMENT ROWS hold the injection column CONVERGED and vary only the
#   tabulation spacing, so the chord error being measured is the traverse chord
#   alone. At the same shipped 1000 ft spacing they report -1.318735072 ft,
#   4.8890e-3 psi, 1.5833e-1 psi and a ratio of 32.386. That is the number that
#   isolates the mechanism.
#
# Both are correct and they answer different questions. Quote either, never both
# in one sentence, and say which one you are quoting. TWO lesson writers hit this
# fork independently and both kept their lessons on one road, which is why it is
# now labelled here rather than left for a third to find.
golden injectionPoint, inputs: surface 1014.7 psia, sg 0.65, transfer differential 100.0 psi, maximum depth 8000.0 ft, wellhead 100.0 degF, bottom 190.0 degF at 8000.0 ft

derived injectionPoint, flowing traverse the tabulation samples. It reproduces every

derived injectionPoint, published curve, so a lesson may quote its numbers only as
derived injectionPoint, a refinement study and never as the golden answer.

# SECTION 22: TEACHING TRAVERSE ON AKASO-3, AND THE CHORD DEFECT AT FULL SIZE
# A TEACHING CONSTRUCT. The published golden traverse is nearly straight, so its
# chord error is small. Real flowing traverses curve, because holdup and friction
# change with depth. This teaching traverse is an explicit smooth curve,
#   p(D) = 144.7 + 0.11 D + 8e-6 D^2 psia, 0 to 7200 ft,
# so its exact crossing against a converged gas column is known to machine
# precision and the tabulation can be coarsened and refined at will. It is NOT a
# published case and NOT a measured traverse. It is here so Expert m04 can show the
# defect at a size a learner can see.
teaching traverse AKASO-3, flowing production pressure at 0.0 ft = 144.700000000 psia
teaching traverse AKASO-3, flowing production pressure at 900.0 ft = 250.180000000 psia
teaching traverse AKASO-3, flowing production pressure at 1800.0 ft = 368.620000000 psia
teaching traverse AKASO-3, flowing production pressure at 2700.0 ft = 500.020000000 psia
teaching traverse AKASO-3, flowing production pressure at 3600.0 ft = 644.380000000 psia
teaching traverse AKASO-3, flowing production pressure at 4500.0 ft = 801.700000000 psia
teaching traverse AKASO-3, flowing production pressure at 5400.0 ft = 971.980000000 psia
teaching traverse AKASO-3, flowing production pressure at 6300.0 ft = 1155.220000000 psia
teaching traverse AKASO-3, flowing production pressure at 7200.0 ft = 1351.420000000 psia
teaching traverse AKASO-3, EXACT crossing against a converged column at 964.7 psia surface with a 55.0 psi transfer differential = 5754.882957403 ft
teaching traverse AKASO-3, exact injection pressure at the crossing = 1097.686548142 psia

# SECTION 23: WHAT THE ENGINE REFUSES TO DO
# Every module that introduces a capability must state its limit. These are the
# engine own refusals, taken from its headers and its code, and every tier needs
# at least one of them.
refusal, the module does not solve the well inflow. There is no IPR anywhere in it.
refusal, the module does not solve multiphase outflow. The flowing production
refusal, traverse used to locate the deepest injection point is PASSED IN as a
refusal, depth-pressure table, so the caller can build it from a validated nodal
refusal, model rather than this module inventing a gradient.
refusal, the unloading and transfer lines are STRAIGHT LINES on constant gradients.
refusal, a real unloading column is neither straight nor constant, and the engine
refusal, does not pretend otherwise, it simply declares the gradient as an input.
refusal, the column is STATIC. There is no friction, no velocity, no injection rate
refusal, in the annulus at all, so the casing pressure it computes is the shut-in
refusal, gas column and not a flowing one.
refusal, intermittent lift is not modelled. Everything here is continuous lift.
refusal, the dome charge z uses Dranchuk and Abou-Kassem with nitrogen criticals,
refusal, which is an extrapolation off the natural gas basis DAK was fitted to. The
refusal, header says so plainly and pins the window it is defensible in, Tpr 2.3 to
refusal, 3.1 and Ppr 1 to 5, and it asserts no agreement with data this repo has not
refusal, verified.
refusal, the throughput is Thornhill and Craver, which is an ORIFICE equation. It
refusal, does not know that a real gas lift valve throttles on its stem before it is
refusal, fully open, so it is an upper bound on what a valve passes and not a
refusal, prediction of it.
refusal, the target depth mandrel is exempt from minSpacingFt. See section 18.
refusal, for a PPO string the closing test is evaluated on the wrong fluid. See
refusal, section 13. This is a PINNED KNOWN DIVERGENCE, not a thing to design on.
refusal, deepestInjectionPoint reports a residual that cannot see its own error. See
refusal, section 20. Treat the residual as a consistency check between two chords.
